# Blackbox Protocol — Skill Notes

Canonical named competency roster. DESIGN-NOTES.md owns learning mechanics and design decisions; this file owns catalogue names and IDs. Publication introduction is not an era lock. Core is inherited by Signal Lost; both are inherited by Dead Zone. General access may require resources/prerequisites; occupational (Professional Learning Tree) access requires credible occupational or qualification gates. All entries have levels I–V. Core conditionally approved subject to occupation and gameplay coverage; expansion selections are working drafts. Exact names and IDs preserved from CCDD-16–18. Semantic overlap, prerequisites, complexity, assessment and occupation mapping remain pending. Ordinary hunting/trapping and wilderness first aid are placed in Signal Lost additions for now, but are learnable in Core campaigns; review whether to relocate to Core during coverage audit without changing totals. No mathematical changes.

| Publication | General | Occupational | Total |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| Core | 200 | 150 | 350 |
| Signal Lost additions | 35 | 15 | 50 |
| Dead Zone additions | 120 | 30 | 150 |
| Total | 355 | 195 | 550 |

## CDR eligibility (approved working rule)

Minimum character CDR is a non-spendable eligibility threshold checked before activating each sequential level. All other requirements remain mandatory. Award incremental CDR only after completion; never deduct it. Documented starting occupational history grants justified levels without retroactive threshold gating; personal purchases must meet thresholds using already validated grants and earlier eligible purchases, not the award from the target itself (implementation default pending review). Thresholds are provisional for playtesting. See DESIGN-NOTES.md CCDD-19 and MANUAL-CHARTS.md.

| Complexity | I | II | III | IV | V |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 1 | 0 | 100 | 500 | 1500 | 3000 |
| 2 | 0 | 200 | 1000 | 3000 | 6000 |
| 3 | 0 | 500 | 2500 | 7500 | 15000 |
| 4 | 0 | 1000 | 5000 | 15000 | 30000 |
| 5 | 2500 | 5000 | 10000 | 30000 | 60000 |
| 6 | 5000 | 10000 | 25000 | 60000 | 120000 |
| 7 | 10000 | 25000 | 50000 | 120000 | 250000 |


## Universal competency modifier (CCDD-20)

User-approved competency modifier: B(L)=5×max(0,L−1), for integer L=0–5. Level I grants no D100 bonus but retains CDR, training and prerequisite value. Each level II–V adds +5 percentage points to the same competency's total (0/5/10/15/20 for I–V). Apply only the highest relevant competency total once per check; never sum separate competencies or previous level totals, never permanently increase underlying general skill. Final possible D100 target remains capped at 99. All other learning mechanics unchanged.

## Record conventions

Draft specification, not playtested. Governing skills/attributes use current pre-audit mapping; remap after audit Section 3. Level I–V respectively fundamentals, basic practical, independent, advanced, mastery. Each level requires prior level, listed supporting prerequisites at I/I/II/III/IV respectively (unless stated otherwise), appropriate access, actual in-world hours, resources, practice and GM-approved assessment. No listed supporting prerequisite means none proposed, not exemption from access or safety. Professional access requires relevant institution, authorized training or documented equivalent; credentials and authority remain separate. CCDD-22 applies to every professional record, including inherited prerequisites: modern institutional names describe a discipline, not a mandatory historical institution. Native and transitional characters may meet the same gate through qualified local mentorship, supervised practice and documented assessment; no automatic levels, CDR or authority. General access is open or resource/prerequisite restricted. Level bonuses +0/+5/+10/+15/+20, highest applicable only. Numeric sequences below are in I–V order: incremental hours, cumulative creation point cost from unlearned, incremental CDR award, cumulative CDR. Upgrade creation cost is difference, not sum. CCDD-12 governs discounts, slots and progress. No clinical or other real-world authorization is conferred. Training hours are game defaults, not professional qualification durations. Assessment rubric is a proposed common rubric: I explain scope and limitations; II demonstrate a routine task with guidance; III independently complete representative task; IV resolve a complex scenario and justify decisions; V demonstrate repeatable expert performance and evaluate alternatives. For each record apply rubric to named discipline and listed resources; hazardous/regulated tasks use simulation or qualified supervision. Detailed discipline-specific assessment cases, mandatory credential hours and alternative routes remain pending.


## Core — General

### G-001 — Culinary fundamentals

Scope: Meal preparation; applies only to tasks within this discipline, not adjacent specialties or independent legal authority. Governing: Crafting (DEX). Complexity 2. Access: general; suitable resources. Supporting prerequisites: none proposed. Resources: kitchen, ingredients and utensils. Assessment: produce a meal meeting stated quality, portion and safety criteria. Apply the common I–V rubric to increasing independence and complexity; record evidence and GM approval.

| III | 1000 | 32 | 8 | 800 | 1400 | +10 |
